### The recorded choices

- **Item 2's home:** `products.py`, as the table says: the merge is a pure function of a state and a record, tested on the fakes without the CLI, and `baseline_record` stays the unmerged state for `configure --products … --apply`'s record (whose before and after must show what the application holds, not what the committed baseline held).
- **Item 3's two constants, whole.** `CREDENTIAL_KEY_PARTS = ("key", "secret", "token", "password", "credential", "cert", "private", "url", "push", "sqs", "sns", "webhook")`: a value under a key whose lower-cased name contains any of them is written as its JSON type, whether it is empty and the SHA-256 of its canonical JSON (sorted keys, no spaces), never the value; a matching key withholds its whole subtree as one digest. The prompt names `certificate`; the constant holds `cert`, which matches `certificate` and also Stream's `p12_cert` (the APN certificate field of its push configuration), a widening, never a narrowing. `VOLATILE_FIELDS = frozenset({"created_at", "updated_at", "duration"})`: the fields the comparison leaves aside, by name, at any depth. Three consequences, recorded: the rule reads the key's name alone, so `revoke_tokens_issued_before` (contains `token`) and a channel type's `url_enrichment` (contains `url`) are digested too, although they are no credential (both still read in clear in `baseline`'s snapshot); the server client's redactor has already replaced the values under the keys it knows before a body reaches the view, so for those the digest is the redaction marker's (said by `redacted_by_the_harness`) and a change of one non-empty value to another is not seen, only a change between empty and non-empty (no such marker occurred in V0: every redactable value was empty); and a boolean has two canonical JSONs, so its digest is reversible (`b5bea41b…` is `true`, `fcbcf165…` is `false`), which withholds a boolean's value in form only.
- **Item 4's commands.** From `services/api`, with uv 0.12.17 from the scratch environment, `UV_CACHE_DIR` under the scratch directory and the proxy and CA names by reference: `uv pip compile requirements-dev.in --python-version 3.12 --generate-hashes --output-file requirements-dev.lock --no-header --constraint ../../proofs/stream-chat/requirements.lock` (`Resolved 45 packages`, exit 0). uv takes the existing output file's pins as preferences, so every package the constraint does not name kept its version. The moved packages, before and after:

| Package | Before (B2's lock) | After (the harness's) |
|---|---|---|
| certifi | 2026.10.23 | 2026.7.22 |
| charset-normalizer | 3.5.2 | 3.5.1 |
| ijson | 3.6.0 | 3.5.1 |
| pydantic | 2.14.1 | 2.13.5 |
| pydantic-core | 2.50.1 | 2.46.5 |
| pydantic-settings | 2.16.1 | 2.15.0 |
| pyee | 15.0.0 | 14.0.0 |
| python-dateutil | 3.0.0 | 2.9.0.post0 |
| python-dotenv | 1.2.4 | 1.2.3 |
| **tzdata** | **2026.5** | **removed** |

  **`tzdata` did not stay as resolved** (a deviation from the prompt's and DM-17 5.1's expectation). B2's lock held it `# via python-dateutil`: `python-dateutil` 3.0.0 requires it, `python-dateutil` 2.9.0.post0 does not, and nothing else in the development set requires it on Linux (`pydantic` 2.13.5 names it only for Windows and its `timezone` extra), so uv dropped the orphan. Keeping it would need a hand edit of the lock or a new pin in `requirements-dev.in`, both forbidden; the premise that `tzdata` is a package only the API holds in its own right was mistaken. It is a development-scope package the runtime lock never had; `pip check` passes and every API check passes without it. The API's virtual environment was reinstalled from the aligned lock (`pip install --require-hashes -r requirements-dev.lock`: the nine packages uninstalled and replaced, `No broken requirements found.`), and the inventory was regenerated from it with a script reading `importlib.metadata` (name, version, scope by lock, `License-Expression`, `License`, the `License ::` classifiers, `License-File`), which reproduces the committed file's 45 unchanged entries exactly.

  **The README's documented command, run as written** (`uv pip compile requirements-dev.in --python-version 3.12 --generate-hashes --output-file requirements-dev.lock --no-header`, without the constraint) on a copy of the aligned lock: `Resolved 45 packages`, and the result is **identical apart from comment lines**: every package line and every hash is the same; the 93 differing lines are uv's `# via` annotations, which in the aligned lock also name `-c ../../proofs/stream-chat/requirements.lock`. So the command reproduces the lock's packages and hashes, and the README is not changed (the prompt forbids it); the constraint belongs in the command the next regeneration uses, or the comment lines drift.

### Reversals (each in a scratch copy outside the checkout; `git status` clean afterwards)

A runner modelled on `checks/fix_reversals.py` (not committed: `checks/` is outside this pass's owned paths) copied `proofs/stream-chat` to a temporary directory, reverted one change at a time with its pattern anchored once at a line start, checked that the reverted module still loads, ran the change's tests with the harness's interpreter (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`), restored the change and ran them again. `reversals: 9, not demonstrated: 0`:

1. item 1, `verify` back to the five default types: `test_a_non_default_type_with_grants_is_a_difference` (`"agent grants for user not empty: […]" not found in […]`) and `test_a_type_stream_adds_later_is_a_difference_once_it_holds_a_grant` (`[] != ["later grants for user not empty: ['read-channel']"]`) fail; restored, OK;
2. item 1, `apply_plan` back to the five default types: `test_the_plan_locks_every_type_the_snapshot_shows` (`['commerce', 'gaming', 'livestream', 'messaging', 'team'] != ['agent', 'commerce', …]`) and `test_plan_enforces_checks_and_locks_everything` fail;
3. item 2, the merge's loop body replaced by `pass`: the four `MergedBaselineTest` tests and `test_record_products_baseline_keeps_the_committed_client_grants` fail (`[] != ['block-user-owner', 'create-call', …]`; the `_changed_paths` list begins with `feeds.feed_visibilities.followers.grants.…`; `'anonymous' unexpectedly found`);
4. item 2, the command writing `baseline_record` instead of the merged record: the `test_cli` test fails;
5. item 3, `withheld` writing every value: `test_credential_like_values_are_written_as_type_emptiness_and_digest` (`'AKIA-not-a-real-key' != {'type': 'string', …}`) and `test_no_credential_like_value_reaches_the_view` (`'AKIA-fake-sqs' unexpectedly found`) fail;
6. item 3, the guard passing every method: `test_the_guard_refuses_anything_but_a_get` (`None != 'full-view sends GETs only; refused POST users/{id}/deactivate'`);
7. item 3, the command not installing the guard: `test_full_view_writes_the_view_under_the_read_only_guard` (`None is not <function read_only_refusal …>`);
8. item 3, the comparison not leaving the volatile fields aside: `test_a_change_in_a_named_volatile_field_is_not` (`[{'path': 'app.body.duration', …}] != []`);
9. item 3, the comparison blind to a type change: `test_a_change_of_type_and_a_digest_change_are_reported`.

Item 4's two reversals are in the table above.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **The live run stopped at item 4.1, as the prompt says it must:** a fifth addition on Stream's side, the Video call type `audio_call`, absent from B2's `baseline` of 14:39:44 UTC and present at 18:28:14 UTC. Class: provider-side configuration drift against the committed products baseline and the lockdown target, of the same kind as D2 and D3: Stream's default client grants (`user` 33, `guest` 6) in a call type the committed baseline does not hold. Its disposition (empty the client roles as D2, and record it in the new baseline as D3) is the manager's and the Dev Manager's, not this session's; `configure --products video,feeds` (a dry run) would plan two `PUT`s now, `agent`'s and `audio_call`'s.
- **Nothing was changed at Stream, in Notion or on GitHub** beyond the branch's three pushes. No departure from the prompt's procedure was made; two outcomes differ from its expectations and are recorded as deviations: `tzdata` left the development lock (item 4, above), and item 2's offline code is committed without the new baseline file.
- **Item 2's offline code committed on its own** (`2f54538`): the prompt puts item 2's code in the same commit as the new baseline file so that no commit names a file that does not exist. The file was never recorded (the stop), so the merge function, the command's use of it, their tests and the README's description are committed with `PRODUCTS_BASELINE` unchanged; no commit names a file that does not exist, and the constant's switch, the fakes' and tests' update and the README's file name wait for the pass that records the baseline. The alternative, leaving tested code uncommitted in an ephemeral container, was judged worse; the manager may drop the commit.
- **No Foundation run on the branch** (row 6). Class: a routing gap between the workflow's push filter and the branch names the session harness assigns (`ccr-…`); the workflow is not owned by this pass and was not changed. The offline checks ran locally with the workflow's commands; the run of record is the manager branch's or PR31's at integration.
- **The chat type `agent`'s stamps.** The snapshot reads `created_at` and `updated_at` 2026-10-23T14:50:33Z for the chat type `agent`, eleven minutes after B2's `baseline`, while B2's record gives `created_at` 2026-10-20T09:15:38Z. The two cannot both describe one object: either Stream re-created the type after B2's read, or B2's stamp was misread; B2's `.work` files were not kept, so it cannot be resolved here. Its roles and grant counts equal B2's (`user` 25, `guest` 21, `anonymous` 0, `channel_member` 22, `channel_moderator` 38, `admin`, `moderator`, `global_admin` and `global_moderator` 43 each, `global_read_only` 4). Class: a discrepancy in the record of a provider-side addition.
- **The two new Video call types are platform defaults.** `agent` and `audio_call` read `created_at` and `updated_at` `0` in Stream's listing, while the four known types read the lockdown's apply time (2026-09-27T05:58:30Z): Stream materializes a default call type at its first write, so the two were never written for this application, and the data holds no time for their appearance. Both read `lifecycle` `{"mode": "single_use"}` where the four known types read `reusable`.
- **`cert` in place of `certificate`** in `CREDENTIAL_KEY_PARTS` (above): a widening of the prompt's list, recorded; and the rule's two over-matches (`revoke_tokens_issued_before`, `url_enrichment`).
- **A boolean's digest is reversible** (above): `async_url_enrich_enabled` reads `true` and `moderation_keyframe_video_enabled` `false` through their digests. The rule withholds a boolean in form only; no credential is a boolean.
- **The comparison did not run live.** V0 was taken once; no second read-only view was taken after the stop, so `compare-views` has no live result and `VOLATILE_FIELDS` is unverified against two live reads (the fakes show `duration`, `created_at` and `updated_at`). The next pass should take two read-only views a minute apart and compare them before its first apply, so that any volatile field Stream carries that the constant does not name is seen on reads, not on an apply.
- **`npm ci`'s advisory** (row 3): B2's finding, named as the prompt asks; no change.
- **`uv`'s deprecation warning** for `UV_NATIVE_TLS` (the environment's variable): trivial, recorded.
- **The `.work` files** (`snapshot-20261023T182816Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T182818Z.json`, `usage-ledger.json`) are in the session's ephemeral container, ignored by git; this section records what they hold.
- **Local tooling:** uv 0.12.17 from the scratch environment; the harness's `node_modules` installed with `npm ci` for the tests; a scratch reversal runner, a scratch inventory generator and two scratch analysis scripts, none committed.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **The stop at item 5.3** (above). Class: an apply's effect beyond its plan, found by the full comparison DM-17 2.3 added for exactly this. Stream's `PUT` of the default chat type `agent` re-created it as an application-level type, with a new `created_at` and the field `blocklist_behavior: "flag"` the request did not send; the nine roles' `scopes` follow the planned grants. Whether a re-created `agent`, with `blocklist_behavior` `"flag"`, is accepted as class 2's lock, and how the rest of B2-C2 (items 6 to 8) runs, are a new decision, not this session's. The development application now holds: the chat target (D1 corrected, the chat type `agent` locked; `configuration.verify`'s chat lines 0 at the apply's re-read), and Video and Feeds as B2-C1 found them (the call types `agent` and `audio_call` with Stream's default `user` and `guest` grants, D2 and D5, open). `conformance` would refuse to start (its `start_refusals`: Video and Feeds 4).
- **For the D8 question** (DM-18 item 8): in this run the first application write to a default type gave it a new creation time: the chat type `agent`'s `created_at` became the apply's second, while the two new call types, which no application write has reached, still read `0` (one observation, not a documented rule). A lock is therefore a write that replaces Stream's default with the application's own type; whether a later Stream re-creation could replace it again is not shown here.
- **No departure from the prompt's procedure.** Section 6 items 1 to 3 are done; item 4 (a rerun fix) was not needed. Nothing was changed at Stream beyond the one chat apply; nothing in Notion; on GitHub only this branch's pushes.
- **The geofences in no fixed order** (item 2): Stream returned the same nine geofences in three orders in three reads (B2-C1's V0, V0a, V0b). Class: provider behaviour against the comparison's positional rule; keyed by name under DM-18 3.3, narrowly (one path; positional fallback).
- **B2-C1's wording on `call_types` and `channel_configs`** (item 3): they are Stream's summaries of the same scopes, not the same objects; B2-C1's statement holds by name. Class: a precision in the record; no change.
- **The full view withholds the feed visibility `private` whole:** its name contains `private`, one of `CREDENTIAL_KEY_PARTS`, so V0a, V0b and V1 hold its type, emptiness and digest only (unchanged across the three). A change there would read as "changed", without the field. Class: an over-match of B2-C1's rule, erring toward withholding (as `revoke_tokens_issued_before` and `url_enrichment`); `products.verify` compares `private` in clear from the snapshot.
- **`compare-views` and `record-products-baseline` need the three names in their environment:** both call nothing, but the harness's `Context` loads the credentials for every command. They ran in the live command form. Class: harness shape, recorded.
- **`npm ci`'s advisory** (check 3): B2's finding, named; no change.
- **The `.work` files** cited here (`snapshot-20261023T210016Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T210018Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T210137Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T211507Z.json`, `compare-views-20261023T210145Z.json`, `compare-views-20261023T210518Z.json`, `compare-views-20261023T211509Z.json`, `configure-20261023T211451Z.json`, `usage-ledger.json`) are in the session's ephemeral container, ignored by git; the digests of withheld values stay there.
- **Local tooling:** a scratch wrapper for the live command form, scratch scripts for the classification, the listings, the plan check and the reversals (scratch copies made with `tar`, `rsync` being absent), all outside the checkout and none committed.

The Video and Feeds lockdown after B2-C2's chat apply, then B2's live rows (the brief, "B2-C2's live run stopped after its chat apply, and B2-C3"; DM-19). **The session did not stop: every step of the prompt's section 5 ran, in order.** Item 1's `baseline` showed exactly the four expected class-3 lines and no chat line; the two read-only views 69 s apart showed no change; the listings showed only the two expected marks; no case of section 3 arose, so no new baseline was recorded and no chat apply ran (V1 is V0b). The products dry run planned exactly the two expected `PUT`s; `configure --products video,feeds --apply` ran once, both `PUT`s 201, its differences after `[]`; the comparison V1–V2 showed 10 fields, every one within section 4 (4 planned grants, 2 derived `scopes` losses, 4 identity fields of DM-19 2.4 on the two call types the write materialized). Both counts were 0 before and after the conformance run. **The conformance run completed: 42 PASS, 2 RECORDED, 2 DIFFERENCE.**

**First, the blocking rule (DM-16 5.2): row D10 differs from the fixture.** "Deactivation of a user Stream does not have" gave `ok` where the fixture gives `member_unavailable`. Under the rule this blocks B2's merge until it is dispositioned. The run's own record shows why: the user the row names (the run's `U9`) **was held by Stream at D10**, because row M5 had just sent a message as `U9` and Stream accepted it (M5's `ok`, the other DIFFERENCE): read with the cleanup's count, that accepted send is what created the user at Stream (an inference from the run's own reads and the adapter's code). So D10 deactivated an existing user, and V13's `member_unavailable` came from Stream's 404 code 16 on that now-deactivated user, not from the adapter's "absent" read: **neither D10 nor V13 exercised "a user Stream does not have" live.** C4, which names the same `U9` before M5, did, through the read `deactivate_user` and `revoke_user_tokens` share. The analysis and a proposal are under "The two differences" below; no code was changed and no rerun was made (the rerun rule covers a run that stopped; this one completed).

The two tables above are the run's: the second lists every Glow code the adapter gave with what decided it (DM-16 4.5); a row whose outcome was `ok` or `already` has no code to map.

**The two differences, and R7.**

- **D10 (blocking; DM-16 5.2): deactivation of a user Stream does not have, `ok` where the fixture gives `member_unavailable`.** The adapter's `deactivate_user` reads the user first (`query_users`, deactivated users included) and raises `member_unavailable` when Stream does not hold it; it sends the deactivation only when the read finds the user (`chat_provider_stream.py`, `deactivate_user`). D10's `ok`, with no status or number, therefore means **Stream held the user at D10**. The user is the run's `U9`, the one identifier the rows use for "a user Stream does not have" (C4, M5, D10 and V13), never provisioned by the run. The cleanup found **one run-prefixed user the run had not recorded** (`users_found_by_prefix_not_recorded` 1; the run records only the four it provisions, `U1` to `U4`), so `U9` existed at the end of the run. Of the requests that name `U9`, in order: C4 (`create_channel`) sent nothing, because its read found `U9` absent (`member_unavailable` by the read; PASS); **M5 (`send_message` with `U9` as the sender) was accepted by Stream**; D10's read then found it; and V13's read found it too, now deactivated, so the adapter sent the revocation and Stream answered 404 with code 16, which the adapter maps to `member_unavailable` (the deactivated-user answer D9 also shows). **Read together, M5's accepted send created `U9` at Stream** (an inference from these reads and the code; Stream's documentation was not consulted). So D10 did not exercise its cause live, and V13's equal code came through another cause (a deactivated user); C4 did exercise "a user Stream does not have", through the same `_users` read that `deactivate_user` and `revoke_user_tokens` begin with. **Under the blocking rule the difference is reported here and blocks B2's merge until it is dispositioned.** A proposal for that decision (not made here; it is code outside this pass's paths unless a rerun is authorized): give each cause row its own never-used identifier (or make M5 after D10 and V13), offline in `conformance.py` with a test, then one more live run of the rows; the alternative is to accept C4's read as the live evidence for the adapter's absent-user path and record D10 and V13 as not exercising their cause.
- **M5: a send by a user Stream does not have, `ok` where the fixture gives `member_unavailable`** (a finding; reported, not fixed). Stream's server-side send accepted a sender it did not hold, into a channel of which that user was not a member, and (above) created the user. For P11's delivery: a send for a sender whose identity is not provisioned is not refused by Stream and creates a bare user outside the provisioning event; only Glow's own checks (D5) and the order of delivery (provisioning before the channel, B1's 3.2) keep that from happening.
- **R7 (recorded either way): a send by a removed member, after the removal: Stream accepted it (`ok`)**; the fixture refuses it (`member_unavailable`). Stream's server-side send does not check membership. As DM-16 4.7 says, this is a reported difference, not a failure: Glow's own send checks refuse it first (D5), and P11's worker must know that Stream will not.
- **V7 (recorded): a token whose `iat` equals the sent cut-off was accepted** (connect and read succeeded), while V6's token, whose `iat` is the cut-off's exact second, one second earlier, was refused (401, code 40). So Stream revokes a token whose `iat` is strictly before the cut-off it holds, and the delivery's rounding of the cut-off up to the next whole second, strictly (F1 point 3; DM-15 5.2), is what refuses a token issued in the bump's own second. V4: the cut-off Stream holds is the exact second plus one, as sent.
- **The signer** (T1): `iat` is the grant's issue time truncated to the second (`iat` − truncated issue 0; `IAT_BACKDATE_SECONDS` 0), `exp` the grant's expiry truncated (`exp` − `iat` 3600), claims exactly `exp`, `iat` and `user_id`, HS256; T2 shows Stream accepts it (connect, and a channel read answered 201). No backdate was needed.

**The provider halves of F3 and CX6** (the blocking rule's rows): C4 `member_unavailable` (PASS, by the read), R8 `channel_unavailable` (PASS, by the read), **D10 `ok` (DIFFERENCE, blocking; its cause not exercised)**, V13 `member_unavailable` (PASS in code, 404 code 16, through a deactivated user; its cause not exercised). Offline, a transport failure and a timeout give `provider_unavailable` (B2's tests, unchanged).

No current path reads it: delivery keeps only the code, the harness keeps code, status and number, and traceback formatting honours the suppression. The module docstring says the chain is dropped, and the test at `tests/test_chat_provider_stream.py:748` asserts only `__cause__`. | **B2-C4.** Raise the mapped error after the `except` block, so `__context__` is `None`, and assert `__context__ is None` in the boundary tests. |

It propagates, and delivery maps it to `provider_unavailable` and retries, then dead-letters after five attempts. Stream's real envelopes carry the fields (M4, D9, V13 mapped). | **B2-C4:** a sentence in the module docstring and a test modelled on the 2xx one. **Recorded here** as the mapping note: the boundary maps only the SDK's own errors (DM-16 4.1), so such a `KeyError` is retryable, as the 2xx case is (DM-17 item 5). |

That second option changes design point d ("removal of both members", DM-16 4.2), so it goes to the Dev Manager first. Not in B2-C4. |

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **No deviation, and no departure stopped at.** `checks/run_plan.py` is unchanged because its count is measured, not written (item 2).
- **A finding outside this work (an operability finding; not a fix-here class: no credential, request outside the guard or provider text is involved).** Cleanup's poll verification (`proof_run.py`, `_verify_polls`) lists polls as each run user that is not deactivated, and M5's sender is now a run user. If a live send did **not** create the sender, that listing asks Stream's Query Polls as a user Stream does not hold. Stream's answer to that is not shown. If Stream refused it, the polls would read "not verified" and `cleanup_problems` would report `remaining_polls: not verified …`: a false problem and exit 4, with every object still deleted. A scratch probe on the fakes, with Query Polls refusing an absent user, showed exactly that line. In the case B2-C3's live run inferred (the send creates the user, AM7-06), the sender exists at cleanup and the listing is as for the other users. Not fixed: cleanup's rules and `proof_run.py` are outside B2-C4. For the manager: either accept it (an exit 4 on that path is diagnosable from the record), or have a later pass skip, in `_verify_polls`, a recorded user that a complete listing does not show, as `_delete_users` already does.
- **Limits.**
  - The new tests run on hand-written fakes and the recorded transport; no row was run live. Whether Stream creates M5's sender stays an inference (AM7-06).
  - `__context__` is `None` when the adapter's caller is not itself handling an exception; otherwise Python sets it to the caller's exception, never to the SDK's (the docstring says so).
  - B2R2 covers `results()`, which feeds the JSON and Markdown records. `cmd_conformance` (`cli.py`, not owned) adds `stop_reason` after `results()` and prints `cleanup:`, `stop recorded:` and `after the run:` lines to the console; those are not generalized by this change. `stop_reason` repeats an exception's redacted text, which `run.stops` (generalized) also holds.
  - uv was installed by version from the package index, not by hash, as in B2-C1.
  - The reversals ran in full scratch copies of the working tree outside the checkout; `git status` was clean afterwards.

### What the exact-head re-review must know

- The adapter's change is confined to where the mapped error is raised (`:140-148`, `:247-257`); `_mapped`, `_send_error`, `_reports_existing`, the caught tuple and every code are unchanged, and `send_message`'s success path moved into the `else` clause unchanged.
- M5's sender is now reserved and recorded (`_new_user`), which adds one user to the conformance count (5) and the reserve (10), and one Query Polls call in cleanup (95 calls on the fakes); see the finding above for the case where the send does not create the user.
- The four keys are `U5` (C4), `U6` (M5), `U7` (D10), `U8` (V13).
- `results()` now generalizes every identifier of the run anywhere in the result; `render()` reads the generalized result.
- The run of record is 40049721647 on `42e6231`; the records commit is Markdown-only.

**The sealed runtime.** `tests/test_chat_sealed_runtime.py` now also requires that the runtime imports neither `glow_domain.chat_reads` nor `glow_domain.chat_projections`, and that no runtime module names them; `glow_chat.reads` was already covered by `glow_chat`.

**The order (DM-22 2.2), chosen and why.** Accepted messages are ordered by `(OutboxEvent.delivered_at, OutboxEvent.sequence)` of their `message_submitted` event. `delivered_at` is the database's `clock_timestamp()` in the transaction that records the provider's acceptance and moves the submission to `accepted`: it is when the message became visible to the other member in Glow's read, so the order **keys "newer" on acceptance**, the first of DM-22's two options, and needs no column. [Corrected by App Manager 7 after C1's exact-head review, finding 3: the guarantee holds only while one channel's receipt transactions commit in their `delivered_at` order, which the in-process deliverer gives today. For P11 this is DM-22 2.2's second case; the P11 plan records the requirement.] `sequence` breaks a tie; neither the app's clock nor a random ID decides. The page, both cursors, the unread count and the read cursor's "forward" use it. `read.order_follows_acceptance` shows it on the database: the first message's first delivery is retried, the second message is delivered first (by a deliverer that does not hold the retried head, as a worker with backoff would; DM-20's note), the reader polls and moves its cursor to the second, then the first is accepted (its `delivered_at` later, its `sequence` earlier, both read back), reaches the reader's next poll and counts as unread. **What it rests on:** one channel's receipts commit in their `delivered_at` order, which holds under the one deliverer at a time the outbox assumes; a deployed worker with concurrent deliverers must keep it (for example, a receipt takes its binding's lock before it reads its time), and an outbox retention rule must keep the delivered `message_submitted` events. Both are for P11 (below). The alternative, relying on per-channel delivery order, was not chosen: it would turn the P11 plan's "Per-channel order across retries" into a requirement for the read, and a retried send would still be delivered after a later one under a worker with backoff.

- **2.2:** `read.order_follows_acceptance`.
- **2.3:** `read.reads_write_nothing` (every row's `xmin` in eleven tables unchanged across a served read, a "nothing new" read, a list, a refused read and a read whose provider call fails).

### Fix reversals

Each reversal ran in a copy of the working tree outside the repository (`/root/c1-reversals`), with absolute paths; the changed file was restored from the copy's base after each. `git status` in the checkout showed only this work's changes throughout, and is clean at the head. **All 33 reversals made their test fail.**

- **Offline (API and harness), 14:** the type and deleted filter removed, a message not asked for kept, an ID answered twice trusted, `member_custom_include` passed, a success without messages read as none, the cap removed, a deleted fixture message returned, the harness's helper without the read (`test_refuse_all` and a stop-type test fail), the digest check removed, delivery projected for another member's message, a reason beside "unavailable", the cursor's canonical re-encoding removed (`test_one_cursor_has_one_text`), the reads imported by the URLs (the sealed-runtime test), a text logged by the adapter.
- **Contracts, 6:** `channel_ref` back on `ChannelEntitlement` (Python, and the regenerated JavaScript validators), `ConversationMessage` open to extra fields (Python and JavaScript), a closed conversation with unread, F11's `read` transition without its conditions.
- **Database, 13** (the read phase from the copy, on a fresh throwaway database each): the final check skipped (every `read_waits`, `check_holds` and `revocation_holds` fails); the provider called inside the first transaction (`read.block_during_provider_call`, `read.provider_error_fails_the_read`); the order by writing (`read.order_follows_acceptance`); the marker over the other member's pending (`read.conversation_list`); unread counting the member's own (`read.unread_and_read_cursor`); the cursor moving back (the same); the cursor's match and binding unchecked (`read.cursor_bound_to_match`); the list without the per-match profile and consent checks (`read.conversation_list`); a provider error giving a page (`read.provider_error_fails_the_read`); a read writing a row (`read.reads_write_nothing`); a text logged (`read.no_text_in_logs`); the reads without the profile and consent checks (all eighteen pause, restriction and withdrawal cases); another member's unsent on a page (`read.pages_and_cursors`, `read.order_follows_acceptance`).

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **No departure stopped at, no migration, no dependency.**
- **Choices for the review** (within the prompt's words, recorded so they can be judged):
  - **`ReadCursorIntent` carries no `CommandMeta`.** The production contracts say every mutating intent carries an idempotency key and expected version; the prompt says to reuse `CommandMeta` "wherever they fit". Here it does not: the command is a monotone maximum ("forward only … a repeat changes nothing"), so a repeat, a replay or two devices racing converge, an expected version would only make two devices conflict, and honouring a key would need `IdempotencyRecord` rows. Recorded in the production contracts' "F11 under OD-45". If the review wants `CommandMeta`, it is a contract change for C1's correction pass, before C2.
  - **F11's registry gains one transition** (`read`, `behind` → `current`), so the oracle does not forbid the command.
  - **`ConversationSummary.state` is `open` or `closed`;** every listed conversation is `open`, and nothing in C1 emits `closed` (it carries no count or marker, for a later push or the app's own state; point d's transport independence).
  - **A page with no accepted message has one check, not two,** since it makes no provider call; its check is its answer, as the list's is.
  - **The member's own failed submissions carry their spool text** while the spool holds it, as pending ones do (the prompt names the pending case only); once the spool is cleared they are unavailable.
  - **Errors:** a deadlock is `state_conflict` (retryable), another database error `state_conflict`; a cursor refusal `stale_version`, as the contracts' "generic stale response".
  - **The order case delivers by event** (`OutboxDelivery._deliver`), to simulate a deliverer that does not hold a retried head; the outbox's own `deliver_due` always holds it.
- **Findings outside the work (none in a fix-here class):**
  - **C1-X1, records (document lag).** The production contracts' "Pagination, idempotency and stale state" still says "The selected provider must prevent direct SDK bypass; A08 remains a required capability proof": under OD-45 no client SDK or token exists. The new "F11 under OD-45" says so; the older sentence is P02's baseline and was left as it is.
  - **C1-X2, tooling (not enforced).** `ruff format --check` over `packages/contracts` reports four Python files unformatted, at the start and at the head (`test_transition_contract.py` among them, before and after this work's added test); CI does not run Ruff there. No change made.
- **Limits, and what goes to P11:**
  - **The text read's provider half is unproven live** (DM-22 8.1): Stream's request shape, its ID limit per call, its answer for an ID it does not hold (an omission or a 404), and whether it keeps text exactly (the read fails closed on any normalization, 2.1). The P11 plan's live row checks these (8.2).
  - **Concurrent deliverers.** The order holds while one channel's receipts commit in their `delivered_at` order; P11's worker must keep that if it runs more than one deliverer, and an outbox retention rule must keep the delivered `message_submitted` events (or a later migration moves the acceptance time onto the submission). A database clock that steps back could misorder two receipts of one channel; `sequence` decides only exact ties.
  - **The list locks every account and match of the member** `FOR SHARE`; with many conversations that is lock traffic for P11's load test (DM-22 item 4's note), and the list is capped at 200.
  - **Indexes.** The reads join submissions to their events by `aggregate_id` and `event_type`; no index serves that join today. P11 sizes it (a migration then).
  - **Uniqueness** of conversations' match IDs and messages' submission IDs holds by construction; the shared key-uniqueness rule covers `items` and `selections` only, so the generated validators do not check it.
  - **The interim rule (3.1)** is built as stated: a conversation leaves the other member's list while one member is paused, restricted, suspended or has withdrawn consent. The A05 question goes to Nathan before P11 serves the reads.
  - Local runs were on PostgreSQL 16.15, not CI's 17.11.

### What the exact-head review must know

- **The safety order** is `glow_chat/reads.py:422-494`: the first check's transaction (`_first_check`), the provider call outside it (`_provider_texts`), the final check in a new transaction (`_final_check`), and only then the page. The planted controls compose the same steps in other orders (`read_phase.py:954-1034`); the adapter has no switch.
- **The checks are the send's,** in the send's order (`_authorize`, `:195`), with the send's own helpers (`:74-76`), under `FOR SHARE`. The list's locks (`:495`) are every account (UUID order), then every match, then the session.
- **The order** is `(delivered_at, sequence)` of each message's `message_submitted` event (`:88`, `:172`, `:350`); read "The order" above for what it rests on.
- **The text check** is `page_messages` (`glow_domain/chat_reads.py:289`) with the send's `request_digest`; the type and deleted filter is in each adapter (`chat_provider_stream.py:338`, `chat_provider_fixtures.py:243`).
- **The contracts:** seven new definitions, `ChannelEntitlement` without `channel_ref`, 213 corpus cases; `ReadCursorIntent` has no `CommandMeta` (a choice for the review, above).
- **The run of record** is [40064952123](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/actions/runs/40064952123) on `91c3945`; the records commit is Markdown-only.

It makes one `get_many_messages` call with `type`, `id` and `ids` only; it keeps an asked ID's text only for a `regular`, undeleted message; it drops any ID answered twice; and an answer without a list of messages raises `provider_unavailable`.

**Observations for the exact-head review** (the manager's, not findings):

1. **The order and concurrent deliverers** (DM-22 2.2). The reads key "newer" and "unread" on `(delivered_at, sequence)`. `delivered_at` is taken before the delivery commits, so two deliveries of one channel's messages could commit out of `delivered_at` order. A reader polling between the two commits would then pass the earlier one, which would never appear and never count as unread. That is the case 2.2 names.
   - The session records the assumption: one deliverer per channel, and the delivered `message_submitted` events kept.
   - **The review judges** whether the read meets 2.2's first case (an order that follows acceptance), or rests on 2.2's second case (per-channel delivery order). The second would make the P11 plan's "Per-channel order across retries" a requirement for the history read, without its "or records the case".
2. **The session's recorded choices,** for the review to judge:
   - `ReadCursorIntent` without `CommandMeta`, against the production contracts' rule for mutating intents;
   - F11's `read` transition;
   - `ConversationSummary.state`'s unused `closed`;
   - one check for a page with no accepted message;
   - own failed submissions' spool text;
   - the error mapping;
   - the order case calling `OutboxDelivery._deliver` directly.
3. **Sixteen revocations.** The brief named nine kinds. The suite's sixteen are those kinds by either member or account (block, unmatch, suspension, deletion, pause, restriction and withdrawal, each by either member; the reader's sign-out and session expiry). Each has three forced cases, for 48 cases. The phase's other 10 are `read.block_during_provider_call` and nine named cases: 58 in all.
4. **The session's findings outside the work:**
   - **C1-X1** (records: the production contracts' older sentence on direct SDK bypass) goes to the review. It is a record claim in a C1-owned file, so a correction pass that follows takes it if the review agrees.
   - **C1-X2** (Ruff's format check over `packages/contracts`, not run in CI, failing at the start too) is a recorded follow-up.
5. **The session's limits** (the text read unproven live; the order's assumptions; the list's shared locks; no index for the submission-to-event join; local runs on PostgreSQL 16.15) are carried to the P11 plan as the session states them.

- the order is DM-22 2.2's second case for P11's purposes (focus area 4).

| # | Severity | Finding | Correction class | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Should fix | `reads.py:133`, `_run`: the reads' transactions open `transaction.atomic()` without `durable=True`. Inside an enclosing atomic block (`ATOMIC_REQUESTS` or a view-level `atomic`, as P11's wiring could add), the first check becomes a savepoint whose `FOR SHARE` locks stay held across the Stream call. A block would then wait on the provider (DM-22 3.2). The proof cannot see it, since it calls the adapter with no enclosing transaction. The manager confirmed the line at `HEAD`; Django is pinned at 5.2.17 | Yes: a safety action could wait on the provider | **To C1-C1:** `durable=True` in `_run`; an assertion that no atomic block is open before the provider call; a read-phase case calling the read inside an open transaction, expecting `error` and no provider call |
| 2 | Should fix | `production-contracts.md:41` says every mutating intent carries an idempotency key and expected version, which `ReadCursorIntent` does not (a choice the review accepts). Line 45's "The selected provider must prevent direct SDK bypass; A08 remains a required capability proof" is superseded by OD-45 (the session's C1-X1) | Yes: a record claim more than the code shows | **To C1-C1:** both sentences, as the review words them |
| 3 | Should fix | The order rests on per-channel receipt order (`reads.py:88`, `:172`, `:315`; `delivery.py:440`, `:458`). `(delivered_at, sequence)` is assigned before the receipt commits, so concurrent deliverers could commit out of that order, and a polling reader would pass a message for good. That is 2.2's case; the in-process deliverer is sequential today | No | **To the P11 plan,** in the review's words, without "or records the case": one channel's receipt transactions commit in `delivered_at` order (for example the binding row locked `FOR UPDATE` before reading `clock_timestamp()`), and delivered `message_submitted` events are retained, or the acceptance time moves onto the submission. The session's label in "Stage C1" is corrected in place |
| 4 | Should fix | `reads.py:510-579`, `_list`: the list locks every account and active match of the member `FOR SHARE` and applies `LIST_LIMIT` only to its output. A member with many matches holds share locks on many other members' rows, so their revocations wait that long (on the database, not the provider; 3.2 holds) | No | **To the P11 plan:** the cap bounds the output, not the locked set; bound the candidates before locking (for example the top 200 by latest activity), one statement per table |
| 5 | Should fix | `read_phase.py:249-412`: no read case distinguishes the final check's lock order. A variant locking session, match, accounts would pass all 58 cases and both controls. The order is right by inspection, and a deadlock maps to a retryable `state_conflict` | No (coverage) | **To C1-C1:** a case holding the read at `after_first_lock` against the suspend and block writers both ways, with a planted variant in another order that must fail it |
| 6 | Nit | `ConversationSummary.state`'s `closed`: nothing emits it | No | **To C1-C1:** record its intended producer (a later push of the same summary, point d) in the F11 section. The contract is unchanged |
| 7 | Nit | `ConversationMessage.at` is the acceptance time for accepted messages and the submission time for the member's own pending and failed ones | No | **To C1-C1:** the production contracts say so, and that a client keys on `submission_id`, never `at`. C2's prompt carries it |
| 8 | Nit | `read_phase.py:264-277`: if a broken adapter held locks across the call, `read_waits` would await the revocation for 60 s and end as a harness error, not its clean signal | No | **To C1-C1:** release the hold first when a wait is observed, as `block_during_provider_call` does |
| 9 | Nit | The reads make no `BINDING_NOT_ACTIVE` check: a `failed` binding under an active match is listed and serves the member's own unsent rows. Harmless, since no accepted message can exist for it | No | **Recorded** in the data model by C1-C1 |
| 10 | Nit | `chat_projections.py:41-50` hard-codes `accountsession_missing`, mirroring the f-string in `_lock_shared` | No | **To C1-C1:** one constant, or a test that ties them |
| 11 | Nit | `FOR SHARE` requests are granted ahead of a waiting `FOR UPDATE`, so continuous overlapping reads of one account row can delay a revocation | No | **To the P11 plan:** an alarm on revocation lock-wait time |
| 12 | Nit | `reads.py:441-442` maps every exception from the provider call to a Glow code, including a programming error or the harness's stop types, as delivery does | No | **To the P11 plan,** for the live row, which calls the adapter directly |

  - `closed`: finding 6.

| Item | Done | Where | Test | Reversal |

| **1, the reads' transactions are durable** (finding 1; DM-22 3.2) | Done. `_run` opens `transaction.atomic(durable=True)`; inside an enclosing atomic block Django 5.2.17 raises `RuntimeError` in `Atomic.__enter__`, before any statement (checked in the pinned source, `django/db/transaction.py:185-193`), and `_run`'s existing handler makes it an `error` result (`reason` `RuntimeError`, no trace), never a page. It covers all three reads. Before the provider call, `read_history` returns `ReadResult("error", "atomic_block_open")` with no provider call if `connection.in_atomic_block` is true: Django lets a durable block nest inside a Django test case's own atomic block, so the assertion holds where the durable flag does not. The cursor move's savepoint (`reads.py:631`, unchanged) stays inside the durable block. **`contact.py`'s `_run` is unchanged** (Stage A's single-transaction design; the review calls its change optional) | `services/api/glow_chat/reads.py:144` (`durable=True`), `:101` (`ATOMIC_BLOCK_OPEN`), `:495-499` (the assertion), `:18-22` (docstring) | API, offline (`tests/test_chat_reads_offline.py`, new, a fresh process under the static registry): `test_a_read_inside_an_open_atomic_block_is_an_error_before_any_statement` (`_run` under an open, non-test-case atomic block: `(None, "error", "RuntimeError", None)`, the body never runs); `test_the_history_read_refuses_an_open_atomic_block_before_the_provider_call` (first check passed, an atomic block open: `error`/`atomic_block_open`, no provider call, projected `state_conflict`; with none open the same read calls the provider once). Database: **`read.inside_open_transaction`** (`read_phase.py:430`): the read inside `transaction.atomic()` on its own connection is `error`, returns no page, makes no provider call, and the enclosing transaction (one transaction: its `now()` unchanged across the read) has **no transaction ID assigned** (`pg_current_xact_id_if_assigned()` is null), so it took no row lock and wrote nothing; the same read outside any transaction returns its page with one provider call | Offline: `durable=True` removed → the first test fails (`(None, 'error', 'ImproperlyConfigured', None)`); the assertion removed → the second fails (a page, the provider called). Four forced waits, each required. **The planted control `read.final_check_lock_order_inverted`**: a read variant whose final check, composed from the adapter's own `_run`, `_lock_shared`, `_signal` and `_authorize`, locks the session, then the match, then the accounts (its first-lock signal after the session's lock); no switch in the adapter. ### Fix reversals

Each reversal ran in a copy of the working tree outside the repository (a scratch directory under the session's scratchpad, made with `tar` from the checkout without `.git`); the changed file was restored from the copy's own original after each, and the copy was removed at the end. `git status` in the checkout showed only this work's changes throughout and is clean at the head. **All ten reversals made their test fail:** five offline (the durable flag, the assertion, the projection's entry, `contact.py`'s reason, the release in `read_waits`) and five on the database (iteration): the durable flag alone, the durable flag and the assertion, the adapter's lock order, and the adapter holding its transaction across the call with and without the release.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **No departure stopped at, no migration, no dependency, no contract shape.**
- **Interpretations, recorded so they can be judged:**
  - **The README's module-table row** (`read_phase.py`'s row, "the two planted read controls" → three) was changed with the read-phase paragraph; the prompt names the paragraph only. Its count would otherwise be false.
  - **The durable flag covers all three reads** (`_run` is shared), not only the history read; the list and the cursor command make no provider call, so for them it changes only that an enclosing transaction is refused.
  - **The new test file** `services/api/tests/test_chat_reads_offline.py` (the owned "tests that cover them"), in the pattern of `test_chat_adapter_offline.py`: a fresh process under the static registry, since `glow_chat` needs the model registry.
  - **The writers in `read.final_check_first_lock`** are `block_by_high` and `suspend_low`: the final check's first lock is the lower (the reader's) account, so a writer whose first lock is that row is the one that meets it both ways; `suspend_high` locks only the higher account and would not meet the final check at its first lock.
  - **The control declares two signals.** Each is deterministic under the inverted variant: a writer arriving while the final check holds only the session's lock is never blocked, and a final check holding the match while waiting for the account the block holds meets the block's match lock.
- **Findings outside the work:** none in a fix-here class. **C1C1-X1, limit (not enforced by code):** the assertion reads Django's `connection.in_atomic_block`, which Django sets for its own atomic blocks; a transaction begun by raw SQL (`BEGIN` through a cursor in autocommit mode) is invisible to both the durable flag and the assertion. P11's wiring opens transactions through Django; the P11 plan may record it.
- **Limits:** the local runs were on PostgreSQL 16.15, not CI's 17.11, and are iteration only. Findings 3, 4, 11 and 12 stay with the P11 plan, as the brief routes them. The text read's provider half stays unproven live (DM-22 8.1).

The database case is `read_phase.py:430`, judged by `pg_current_xact_id_if_assigned()` inside the enclosing transaction.
- **Finding 5** is `read_phase.py:464-547`; the control's variant is `:1143`, composed from the adapter's `_run`, `_lock_shared`, `_signal` and `_authorize`; the adapter has no switch.
- **Finding 8** is `read_phase.py:285-288`.
- **Finding 10** is a test (`test_chat_reads_offline.py:116`); `chat_projections.py` is unchanged.
- **The run of record** is [40109633125](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/actions/runs/40109633125) on `f4749ee`; the records commit is Markdown-only.

That row is outside the read-phase paragraph the prompt names. **Accepted:** left alone, its count would be false, and the brief's revision 14 owns the README's read-phase lines for this reason.

  - `read_history` returns `error`, `atomic_block_open`, before the provider call when `connection.in_atomic_block` is true (`:495-499`). Its comment records why that guard is needed beside the durable flag: Django lets a durable block nest inside a test case's own atomic block.

It goes to the P11 plan: P11 opens every transaction through Django, never raw `BEGIN`, on a connection the reads use. ### The approved departure (R1)

The prompt asked for the guard before the provider call (`reads.py:495`) to refuse `connection.in_atomic_block or not connection.get_autocommit()`. It also asked for a database case that shows no transaction ID assigned and a block not held up, and for a reversal of that guard alone that fails the case. On the local database, at the start's code, a read under `transaction.set_autocommit(False)` returned its page with one provider call and a transaction ID assigned (R1 reproduced). With only the extended guard (in a scratch copy) it returned `error` with no provider call, but **a transaction ID was assigned**: the first check had already taken its `FOR SHARE` locks inside the caller's transaction, and they stay held until the caller ends it. So the case's "no transaction ID" and "a block not held up" could hold only if the read refused before its first statement, and then the prompt's reversal could no longer fail the database case. The session stopped before R1's code and asked, through Nathan.

**The manager's direction** (App Manager 7, recorded in the brief at `09d8fe4`): an approved departure from the prompt's letter, not a design change, with no Dev Manager read. `_run` refuses before any statement, and before it enters its durable block, whenever the caller has a transaction open (`connection.in_atomic_block or not connection.get_autocommit()`, checked outside the block, since inside any atomic block autocommit is off by design), with one Glow reason, for all three reads. The durable flag stays, and so does the guard before the provider call, extended as specified, as a second line. The reversals are the manager's: `_run`'s check reverted fails R1's and R3's cases; `_run`'s check and the guard both reverted fail R1's case by a provider call; the guard alone reverted fails its offline test.

### R2: the order after the first lock

`read.final_check_first_lock` pins the final check's **first** lock (the lower account's, whoever reads); it does not pin the order after it. The second wrong order (the lower account, the match, the higher account, the session) passes it, and it is deadlock-free against today's writers. Checked against `services/api/glow_chat/contact.py` at the head:

- **Each writer that locks both accounts takes the lower first:** `send` (`:465-469`: lower, higher, match, session), `block` (`:564-576`: lower, higher, then the match and the block row), `unblock` (`:631-635`), `unmatch` (`:677-680`: lower, higher, match), and `activate_match` (`:243-245`).
- **None locks the higher account or the match without the lower:** the match is locked only by `send`, `block` and `unmatch`, each after both accounts. The single-account writers (`_revoke_account` `:718`, `_set_profile` `:799-801`, `_decide_consent` `:848`, `open_session` `:379`) lock one account row and their own profile or consent rows, which the reads do not lock. `_end_session` (`:414`) locks the session row only. `grant_token` (`:332-336`) takes shared locks only. `delivery.py` locks bindings, identities, submissions and events, none of which the final check locks. [Corrected by App Manager 7 after C1-C2's exact-head re-review, N1: four writers lock the higher account alone when they act on the higher member (`_revoke_account` `:718`, `_set_profile` `:799`, `_decide_consent` `:848`, `open_session` `:379`), so read this as "none locks the match, or both accounts, without taking the lower account first". The single-account writers are handled in the sentences that follow, and the deadlock-free conclusion holds, which the re-review checked against `contact.py`.]
- **So no cycle:** the final check holds the lower account `FOR SHARE`. Any writer that holds the higher account or the match `FOR UPDATE` must already hold the lower account, which conflicts with the final check's share lock. They cannot both hold it, so the final check never waits on such a writer while it holds the lower account. A writer that holds only the higher account, or only the session, waits on nothing the final check holds. Shared locks never wait on one another. [Corrected by App Manager 7, N1: read "any writer that holds the match, or both accounts, `FOR UPDATE` must already hold the lower account".]

The reader's-own-account-first order is the one this case now catches: for the higher member's read it locks the higher account before the lower, which a block's lower-then-higher order meets in a cycle.

- **offline, four:** the guard alone; `_run`'s check; the check outside the `try`; the new proof tests against the start's `read_phase.py`.

The two R2 orders against the start's case both passed, confirming the re-review's R2 finding. Against the new case, the reader's own account first fails and the second order passes, as recorded.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **One approved departure:** R1's refusal in `_run` (the manager's direction, above). No other departure, no migration, no dependency and no contract shape.
- **The correction of C1C1-X1's claim.** C1-C1's section says the assertion "reads Django's `connection.in_atomic_block` … a transaction begun by raw SQL (`BEGIN` through a cursor in autocommit mode) is invisible to both the durable flag and the assertion". That named the wrong path and claimed more than the code showed. Per C1-C1's re-review, the pinned psycopg 3.3.6 refuses a raw `BEGIN` there (`ProgrammingError`, no provider call), which this session did not repeat. The open path was Django's own `transaction.set_autocommit(False)`, which is not an atomic block, so it passed both the durable flag and the assertion, and the first check's locks were held across the provider call (R1, reproduced here at the start's code: a page, one provider call, a transaction ID assigned). Since C1-C2, `_run` refuses before any statement while the caller has a transaction open through Django, an atomic block or autocommit off (item 1). C1-C1's section is unchanged.
- **Interpretations, recorded so they can be judged:**
  - **The guard's reason** is `atomic_block_open` when an atomic block is open (C1-C1's, unchanged) and `transaction_open` when only autocommit is off. `_run`'s single reason is `transaction_open`.
  - **R2's offline test** replaces the two hold helpers and checks the calls; the waits themselves are the database's (check 7).
  - **`read.autocommit_off`** first reads with autocommit on (a page, one provider call), so that the case shows the world is readable and that the later provider-call count is unchanged.
  - **No database-side check** before the provider call (item 1).
- **Findings outside the work:** none in a fix-here class.
- **Limits:**
  - The local runs were on PostgreSQL 16.15, not CI's 17.11, and are iteration only.
  - `_run`'s refusal covers transactions Django knows about; the driver's own raw `BEGIN` path was not re-tested here (the re-review's limit stands).
  - R4 (serving the reads) stays with the P11 plan, as the brief routes it.
  - The text read's provider half stays unproven live (DM-22 8.1).

The database case is `read_phase.py:474`, judged by `pg_current_xact_id_if_assigned()` inside the caller's transaction and by `pg_stat_activity`, `pg_locks` and `pg_blocking_pids` for the block. Every read now refuses inside any atomic block before the durable flag is reached, so `read.inside_open_transaction` records `transaction_open`.

### The design, the routes and the module

- **The adapter interface:** `ChatTransport` (`src/chat/transport.ts:13`): `conversations()`, `messages(MessagePageRequest)`, `send(MessageIntent)`, `readCursor(ReadCursorIntent)`, each resolving with the untrusted response body. C2's only implementation is the in-process fixture; there is no network client, API URL or route. P11 connects the interface to Glow's API with `fetch`.
- **The fixture adapter:** `FixtureChatAdapter` in `src/chat/fixture-adapter.ts:83`, composed in `src/chat/context.ts:24` (`chatFor`) beside P05's interactions fixture, as `src/interactions/context.ts` composes P05's.
- **The client boundary:** `ChatClient` (`src/chat/client.ts:45`).
- **The presentation store:** `ChatStore` (`src/chat/store.ts:59`), with the intervals as constants (`:6`, `:8`).
- **The screens:** `src/chat/screen.tsx` (`ConversationsScreen` `:26`, `ConversationScreen` `:81`, the composer `:68`); the labeled fixture controls `src/chat/controls.tsx`; Glow's wording `src/chat/copy.ts`.
- **The routes:** `/conversations` (`src/app/conversations.tsx`) and `/conversation` (`src/app/conversation.tsx`), fixed and query-free, in `SAFE_ROUTES` (`src/onboarding/routes.ts:7`), guarded in `src/app/_layout.tsx:49` by `canAccessRoute` (`routes.ts:32`: a current active account only). Reachable from a match: **Open conversation** on an active match's detail (`src/interactions/screen.tsx:75`) and **Your conversations** on **Your matches** (`:50`).

**The choices made** (within the prompt's words, recorded for the review):

- **Responses as bodies, validated at one boundary.** The fixture returns the JSON bodies Glow's API will return, an `AppResponse` envelope or an `Error`; `ChatClient.accept` (`client.ts:51`) runs `parseAppResponse` (the generated `validateAppResponse` and `assertUniqueKeys`), then the DTO's own generated validator, the expected `kind` and the request's `match_id`. Anything else, including a valid `Error` body that is not answered as one, is refused whole. Because the shared key-uniqueness rule covers `items` and `selections` only (the production contracts' "Uniqueness"), the client also refuses a page naming a `submission_id` twice and a list naming a `match_id` twice: the app keys messages on `submission_id`. `src/contracts/validation.ts` is unchanged, so the shared rule stays identical to Python's.
- **Point c in the fixture.** Every request makes the interactions fixture's own contact check (`FixtureInteractionAdapter.contact(matchId).eligible`: the match active and its pair current), so an unmatch, a block either way, a pause, a withdrawn consent, a policy or eligibility change and the other member's suspension or deletion all refuse the list, the page, the send and the cursor (`fixture-adapter.ts:171`, `grant`). The member's own session gone is `unauthenticated`; the member's own account suspended or deletion-pending is the generic `not_found`, as C1's projection maps `account_not_active`. No member-state model was added.
- **"Each member the fixture can show."** The fixture shows the signed-in member through the transport and fictional Jules (and, for one test, Iris) through `developmentMember` (`fixture-adapter.ts:328`), answered by the same checks. A fictional member is shown only inside the signed-in member's session, as P05's `developmentSession` is.
- **Order and cursors.** Accepted messages come in acceptance order (a fixture clock tick at acceptance), with C1's page algorithm: the newest page, then pages after `newer_cursor` or before `older_cursor`, the member's own pending and failed submissions on a page at the head only. A page cursor is `c1.<o|n>.<match>.<submission>` in hex, opaque to the app, refused as `stale_version` unless it names an accepted message of the requested match (`:50`, `:236`).
- **Polling.** `CONVERSATION_POLL_MS = 3_000` and `CONVERSATION_LIST_POLL_MS = 30_000` (`store.ts:6`, `:8`), on an injected timer (fixture time in the tests) and React Native's `AppState` (`context.ts:15`): only `active` is the foreground, so iOS's transitional `inactive` polls nothing either. Leaving a screen clears its interval; returning, or returning to the foreground, reads once and restarts it. A change in Glow's match projections (the interactions fixture's change signal) reads the selected conversation and the shown list at once in the foreground, so a hidden, retained conversation is revoked promptly; in the background it waits for the foreground (`store.ts:129`).
- **The send's `expected_version`** is the current match projection's `version` (`src/chat/authority.ts`), which equals the fixture's contact version; the contracts do not name which projection carries the contact version (a question for P11, below).
- **Retry.** The store keeps the parsed `MessageIntent` and sends the same object again (`store.ts:287`); an `offline`, a retryable temporary error and an unconfirmed (`invalid_response`) send are retryable; a refusal is final. Keys are `chat-message:<serial>-<random>`.
- **The read cursor** (`store.ts:243`) moves to the newest accepted message, forward only, only after a newest or newer page added the other member's accepted messages while the conversation is shown in the foreground. Older pages, own messages and polls with nothing new never move it.
- **Revocation** (`store.ts:260`): any `unavailable` or `signed_out` refusal drops the history, unsent rows, draft and cursors, removes the conversation from the list, shows **This conversation is unavailable.** and stops its polling. Another account, sign-out or a restricted account clears everything (`store.ts:134`).
- **The page size** the app asks for is `CONVERSATION_PAGE_LIMIT = 20` (`store.ts:10`); the contract allows 1 to 50.
- **One inherited rendered assertion changed.** `rendered/interactions.spec.ts`'s test "two committed fictional likes … with no messaging grant" asserted `Messaging is unavailable` on an active match's detail; item 5 now gives an active match a conversation. The test (renamed "… whose messages open only on the guarded conversation route") asserts the new sentence, that **Open conversation** is shown, that the detail has no composer, and keeps every other assertion (no private field; no "send message" or "start chat" button on the match screen). The restricted and suspended details still say `Messaging is unavailable`, and the suspended test's assertion is unchanged. No other inherited test changed.

### Fix reversals

Each reversal ran in a copy outside the repository (`$scratchpad/rev/apps/mobile`, with `packages/contracts/fixtures` and `production` beside it and `node_modules` linked to the checkout's), with absolute paths; the changed file was restored from the checkout after each, and the copy's 65 chat unit tests passed before and after the series. *(Manager's note, from the exact-head review's finding 2: the head has 67 chat unit tests, which is 583 minus 516. Which two the copy did not run is not recorded.)* **All 36 reversals made their tests fail.** `git status` in the checkout showed only this work's changes throughout.

- **Item 1 (11):** order by writing (the 2.2 tests in the adapter and the store); the other member's unsent on a page (F11); unread counting the member's own (6.3); the marker over the other member's pending (6.1); the cursor moving back; the cursor to an unaccepted message (point e); the page cursor not bound to its match (6.4); text shown without the digest check (2.1, and the store's "unavailable" test); an empty page failing the text read (2.4); a replay deduplicated before authorizing; no idempotent replay (the adapter's and the store's lost-response test).
- **Item 3 (5):** no contact check (twelve revocation and store tests); a member of another match acting in this one; the member's own inactive account answering like an active one; a revocation keeping the history; another account keeping the conversation.
- **Item 2 (5) and item 10 (1):** the envelope not validated; the DTO kind not checked; a page of another match accepted; a submission twice accepted; an intent sent without `parseAppIntent`; and both validation layers removed, which fails 16 of the 18 display-rule tests (the error-text case is still refused, as an `Error` body with an extra field, on its own path; the "unavailable" wording test does not depend on validation).
- **Item 9 (6):** each interval changed; polling ignoring the background; a match change in the background reading at once; polling after the screen is left; the list polling after it is hidden.
- **Item 5 (3):** newer reads without the page's newer cursor; messages keyed on `at`; older pages without the page's older cursor.
- **Item 8 (2):** the cursor moving when not shown; the cursor moving on every read.
- **Item 4 (2):** a retry building a new intent with a new key; an unconfirmed send not retryable.
- **Item 7 (1):** the chat routes open to a restricted account.

One line was removed instead of reversed: a check of the fictional member's own account in `member()` that no test could fail, because the interactions fixture's contact check already refuses that member's suspension, deletion or revoked session. The membership check that does distinguish members is reversed above.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **No departure stopped at; no contract, dependency, workflow or service change.** The one change to an inherited test is recorded under "The choices made".
- **Findings outside the work:** none in a fix-here class.
  - **C2-X1, fixture fidelity (recorded, no change).** The interactions fixture's pair check treats the other member's revoked session as ineligible, so the chat fixture closes a conversation for both members when one member's session ends (`fixture-adapter.test.ts:224`). C1's reads check only each requester's own session and the other account's state. The fixture fails closed, so nothing is shown that C1 would refuse; it can refuse what C1 would serve.
  - **C2-X2, a question for P11.** The production contracts name no projection that carries a match's contact version, which a `MessageIntent`'s `expected_version` must equal. The fixture uses the match projection's `version`, which equals its contact version; P11 states which value a client sends.
- **Limits:**
  - **No transport exists.** The screens are proven against the in-process fixture only. P11's `fetch` transport, its status-code mapping, timeouts and the served reads (and DM-22 8.1: the history read's provider half is unproven live; serving it waits on the P11 plan's live row) are unproven.
  - **The fixture is a presentation substitute.** It applies C1's semantics in TypeScript; the Python reads in `services/api` stay authoritative. Its point-c checks are the interactions fixture's, not C1's locks and two checks around a provider call.
  - **Background behavior** is shown on fixture time with an injected app state. React Native's `AppState` on devices, iOS's `inactive` and the rendered harness's page visibility are not exercised natively; native builds, push (P06.3), keyboard behavior and VoiceOver/TalkBack are unverified.
  - **Retained history is not bounded** in the store; the fixture bounds a session at 1,000 submissions.
  - **The rendered provider-field journey covers five of the fifteen cases** (and the list); the unit tests cover all fifteen in every response kind.
  - **Local rendered runs used Chromium 1243, not the pinned 1234**; the pinned-browser evidence is the run of record's.

### What the exact-head review must know

- **The boundary** is `ChatClient.accept` (`src/chat/client.ts:51`); nothing reaches `ChatStore` except through it, and the screens render only `ChatStore`'s state and Glow's match projections (names).
- **The fixture's point-c check** is `grant` (`src/chat/fixture-adapter.ts:171`), which defers to `FixtureInteractionAdapter.contact`. No new member-state model.
- **Polling and the foreground** are `schedule`, `activityChanged` and `authorityChanged` (`src/chat/store.ts:150`, `:117`, `:129`); the intervals are `:6` and `:8`.
- **The revoked state** is `revoke` (`store.ts:260`); a revocation drops history even for a retained, hidden screen.
- **The no-provider test** is `src/chat/no-provider.test.ts`; its patterns are deliberately built from fragments.
- **The one inherited test change** is `rendered/interactions.spec.ts:73` (above).
- **The run of record** is [40135312825](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/actions/runs/40135312825) on `cb3a6a6`; the records commit is Markdown-only.

- **Branch and heads.** `claude/p06-2-stage-c2-37vrqt`: code commit `cb3a6a6dae0f3ad629a1cbd92ea299fcd5cab03a`, records commit `676b49b639919d243711e14613f209c7d3d7f2ab` (tree `ad7b4be`), both as reported. The prompt's commit `b23253b` is their parent.
- **Changed paths.** 24 against `b23253b`, every one inside C2's owned paths: 23 code paths under `apps/mobile/src/` and `apps/mobile/rendered/`, and `apps/mobile/README.md` (2,266 lines added, 7 removed). The records commit changes only this file. `git diff --check` is clean.
- **Classification** (the trusted policy from `main` at `a8e9528`, run outside the tree): `"full": true`, `behavior-or-empty`.
- **Unchanged:** an empty diff from `b23253b` over `apps/mobile/package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `.npmrc`, `dependency-inventory.json`, `src/contracts/generated/`, `app.config.ts` and `scripts/development.mjs`, and over `packages/`, `services/`, `proofs/`, `.github/` and `scripts/`.
- **The evidence record:** the first 619,444 bytes at `676b49b` equal the whole file at `b23253b`, so the earlier sections are byte-identical and "Stage C2" is appended.
- **What the manager read in the code:**
  - `store.ts` sets `CONVERSATION_POLL_MS = 3_000` and `CONVERSATION_LIST_POLL_MS = 30_000`;
  - no `fetch(`, `new WebSocket`, `new EventSource` or `XMLHttpRequest` appears in the app's non-test source;
  - `no-provider.test.ts` builds its package and construction patterns from fragments, so that it does not match itself, and scans the manifest, the lock and the app's source;

  - the changed inherited assertion (`rendered/interactions.spec.ts`, the active match's detail) now asserts the new wording, the "Open conversation" button and that the detail has no composer.
- **Run of record.** Foundation push run 40135312825 on `cb3a6a6`.

- **The session's findings:**
  - **C2-X1** (the fixture closes a conversation for both members when the other member's session is revoked; C1 checks only the requester's own session): a fixture-fidelity limit, stricter, never looser. The exact-head review judges whether it shows one member another's sign-out in the fixture.
  - **C2-X2** (no contract names the projection carrying the version a send's `expected_version` must match): the server's send carries the domain's `contact_version` (`SendCommand.contact_version`), and neither `MatchProjection` nor `ChannelEntitlement` names it as such. The manager proposes it as a contract question for P11, before the send is served, and the P11 plan carries it. The review judges it.

## Stage C2-C1

### Start gate

`git fetch origin claude/magical-wozniak-yfmmx2 main`; `git merge --ff-only e578b08…` on the session branch; `git rev-parse HEAD` printed `e578b08cccb25b8767584a5540a7836cf29636be`; `git merge-base --is-ancestor a8e9528… HEAD` printed `C1 present`; `git diff --quiet cb3a6a6… HEAD -- apps packages services proofs .github scripts` printed `C2's code`; `git diff --stat e578b08… origin/claude/magical-wozniak-yfmmx2 -- apps/ packages/ services/ proofs/ .github/` printed nothing; `git show origin/claude/magical-wozniak-yfmmx2:docs/testing/evidence/2026-10-05-p06-2-chat-integration.md | grep -c '^## Stage C2-C1'` printed `0`. The reading list was read before the first change. As `apps/mobile/AGENTS.md` asks, React Native's ScrollView page (0.87; `onScroll`'s `contentOffset` and `layoutMeasurement`, `scrollEventThrottle`) and its layout-measurement page were read, with the installed type definitions of `measureLayout` (`react-native` 0.86.3: coordinates "relative to the origin x, y of the ancestor view") and React Native Web 0.21.0's `onLayout` and `measureLayout` sources.

### The visibility mechanism, and why

**Visibility tracking on the message list, computed from the measured layout** (DM-23 2.1's first form), with React Native's own interfaces only:

- **The conversation's own scroll container,** `ConversationPage` (`src/chat/screen.tsx:132`): `Page`'s structure (safe area, keyboard avoidance, the same `ScrollView` props), plus the scroll view's `onLayout` (the visible area's height) and `onScroll` (`contentOffset.y` and `layoutMeasurement.height`, `scrollEventThrottle={16}`), with the content in one view whose origin is the scroll content's origin. **`Page` (`src/components/ui.tsx`) is unchanged,** so every other screen is too.
- **Each row is measured** with `measureLayout` against that content view (`screen.tsx:109`), after every render (`:114`) and every size change of the content (`ConversationPage`'s content `onLayout`). Each pass starts empty, so a row not yet measured in the current pass is never in view.
- **The rule** (`src/chat/visibility.ts:16`): a row is in view when its whole box lies inside the visible area, within one pixel of rounding, or, for a row taller than the area, when it fills the area. An unmeasured row or area is never in view.
- **The report** (`useViewedMessages`, `screen.tsx:96`): the IDs of the rows in view go to `ChatStore.viewed` on each measurement, scroll and layout event, and when the screen gains focus. The screen sends nothing itself.

**Why this mechanism:**

- **Not a `FlatList` with `onViewableItemsChanged`:** the conversation's composer, controls and navigation share its scroll; a virtualized list would need them as header and footer, and virtualization would change which rows the inherited rendered tests can count.
- **Not cached `onLayout` positions alone:** React Native Web reports `onLayout` through a `ResizeObserver`, so on size changes only. A row that moves without resizing, such as every row when an earlier page loads above it, would keep a stale position and could be counted in view before it is. Hence the re-measurement after every render, which the rendered test `chat.spec.ts:108` covers (reversal RR4).
- **Not scrolling to the newest message on opening:** DM-23 2.2 allows it but does not require it. The screen opens at the top, as before; a member reaches the newest message by scrolling. Recorded as a limit.

### The store's cursor path

- **`viewed(submissionIds)`** (`src/chat/store.ts:129`): ignored while the conversation is not shown. Otherwise it keeps the report and, in the foreground, moves `seen` forward to the newest *accepted* message in it (`noteSeen`, `:138`), never backward. An unsent message never counts.
- **`moveReadCursor`** (`:280`) sends `ReadCursorIntent` to `seen` only when `seen` is past the last cursor the store moved and the messages between include one from the other member; only in the foreground; at most one in flight. A move made while one is in flight follows it once it succeeds (`:298`). A command that fails without ending access is tried again after the next successful read (`:226`), not on each report.
- **A page arriving never moves it** (`:226`): the page's only effect is that retry. Older pages, the member's own messages and polls move nothing.
- **The foreground:** a report made in the background counts when the app returns, if the screen is still shown, and a deferred move is sent then (`:155`). Leaving the screen clears the report (`:121`); a revocation, another conversation or another account clears `seen` too.
- **One behaviour changed beyond the cursor's target:** a move to a message that was in view may now complete after the screen is left; before, `moveReadCursor` required the screen to be shown. The message was in view while shown and in the foreground, so 2.1 holds, and a fast exit no longer leaves a seen message unread.

  - **A message is in view only when wholly inside the visible area** (or filling it), within one pixel. A partly visible message is not counted.
  - **Every row is measured after every render.** Retained history is not bounded in the store (Stage C2's limit), so a very long retained conversation measures many rows per render.

## Codex's code review of C2-C1's head (25 October 2026)

- **The review:** Codex's code review of `73ef97e`, triggered by the new commits, started at 00:22:36 UTC and posted one finding at 00:25 UTC. Its review of `83ce64d`, a records-only commit, started at 00:24:59 UTC.
